# Groundwork — Property Acquisition & Underwriting

A single-operator workspace for residential property leads, local market signals, and rental underwriting. The initial release is designed for national data models with Arkansas, Alabama, Ohio, Missouri, and Texas prioritized in the workflow.

## What works

- Add a conventional, tax-sale, code-enforcement, foreclosure, or pre-foreclosure lead manually; retain its source URL and evidence for supplied metrics.
- Edit existing opportunities without losing unsubmitted fields; new values append source-attributed metric evidence to the ledger.
- Import property records from UTF-8 CSV. Required columns: `address`, `city`, and two-letter `state`. Other accepted columns use the field names in `backend/app/schemas.py`.
- Search and filter the pipeline by state and maximum asking price, view geocoded properties on an OpenStreetMap-backed map, and inspect a source-aware property detail view.
- Calculate tax from a supplied annual bill or from assessed value × supplied tax rate. There is no universal insurance formula: enter a quote or a clearly attributed user estimate.
- Compare market-rent inputs with Section 8 screening benchmarks from HUD Fair Market Rent and PHA payment-standard inputs. A PHA must be marked confirmed before the Section 8 underwriting scenario is shown.
- Keep program participation, waitlist status, and open voucher counts separate. A missing count remains unknown; it is never derived from a waitlist status.
- Retrieve county population estimates from the Census Bureau ACS 5-year API using a five-digit county FIPS code. The displayed annual growth rate is annualized from the configured vintage and the vintage five years earlier.
- Refresh saved Census population metrics daily for properties with county FIPS codes. With a HUD USER API key, refresh ZIP- and bedroom-specific Fair Market Rent benchmarks daily as well.
- Configure recurring Socrata, ArcGIS FeatureServer, public JSON API, direct CSV, CSV-in-ZIP, or selectable-text PDF feeds for jurisdiction records. Map address-level or area-level data into the property pipeline, or retain unmodified sale-list rows and PDF pages as source records.
- Browse all 599 Census county-equivalents in Arkansas, Alabama, Ohio, Missouri, and Texas (including St. Louis city and all 254 Texas counties), find assessor/tax-sale/code-enforcement/foreclosure/pre-foreclosure source-discovery links by county, and see whether a feed is configured, paused, or still needs a verified endpoint.
- Inspect sync history, per-source errors and skipped records, and the next scheduled run; queue a manual run from **Data sources**.
- Score available yield, population-growth, and crime-safety inputs with configurable weights. The response exposes formulas and data coverage; missing inputs are excluded and not treated as zero.
- Keep evidence sources separate by property lead, tax, insurance, market rent, HUD, PHA, and neighborhood metrics; display the recorded source, confidence, and capture date in the evidence ledger.
- Find nearby geocoded opportunities. PostgreSQL uses PostGIS and a GiST spatial index; local SQLite development uses a great-circle distance calculation.

## Daily data automation and source setup

The API starts a daily scheduler at **02:00 America/Chicago** by default. Configure the schedule before starting the API:

```powershell
$env:DAILY_SYNC_ENABLED = "true"
$env:DAILY_SYNC_TIME = "02:00"
$env:DAILY_SYNC_TIMEZONE = "America/Chicago"
$env:CENSUS_ACS_YEAR = "2024"
```

`DAILY_SYNC_TIME` uses 24-hour `HH:MM` local time and `DAILY_SYNC_TIMEZONE` must be an IANA time-zone name. Set `DAILY_SYNC_ENABLED` to `false` to disable the daily timer; the run-now executor remains available. The database enforces a single active run and a unique scheduled run per local calendar date, so multiple API workers do not duplicate the daily execution. For production, use PostgreSQL rather than SQLite when running multiple workers.

Set `HUD_API_KEY` to enable the official HUD USER FMR API refresh. `HUD_FMR_YEAR` optionally pins a Fair Market Rent data year; otherwise the current calendar year is requested. HUD enrichment requires both a five-digit ZIP code and a known bedroom count. FMR is a screening benchmark, not the administering PHA's payment standard or a promise of rent approval.

The **Data sources** view can register Socrata, ArcGIS FeatureServer, JSON API, direct CSV, CSV-in-ZIP, and selectable-text PDF feeds. Choose **Property leads** to map upstream address, city, and state fields; add a stable record ID field when available. Choose **Area enrichment** to match existing properties by county FIPS, ZIP plus state, or county plus state and refresh only area-level population, crime, rent, or PHA metrics. Choose **Source records** for sale-list rows or PDF pages whose fields should be retained as published rather than mapped into property fields; use **View latest** to inspect the most recently downloaded snapshot. CSV/ZIP feeds accept up to 100,000 rows and downloads up to 250 MB (the uncompressed CSV is also capped at 250 MB); PDF feeds accept up to 500 text-extractable pages and 25 MB, with no OCR. API connectors retain their existing 5,000-record and 10 MB response-page caps. A feed's refresh interval is 7 days by default and can be set from 1 to 365 days. Manual runs ignore the interval, but do not bypass source authorization or access controls.

Feed creation requires an explicit confirmation that the source permits recurring automated downloads. This is an operator attestation, not a terms review or legal determination: verify the source's terms, licensing, cadence, and retention rights first. Do not configure restricted sources, bypass access controls, or evade bot blocks. Optional server-side API credentials are specified by environment-variable *name* in the feed configuration (for example, `TAXLIENSIMPLE_API_KEY`) and are never stored in the database; select a query-parameter name only for APIs that require API keys in query strings. For Socrata, `SOCRATA_APP_TOKEN` is optional. JSON API pagination expects `limit`/`offset` parameters when the provider supports them.

Examples of the supported feed patterns:

- **Socrata:** use the jurisdiction's dataset domain and 4-by-4 dataset ID, then map its address/city/state, parcel ID, assessed value, tax bill, code case, or crime-rate fields.
- **ArcGIS:** use the public FeatureServer layer URL (not the dashboard page); map the layer's attribute fields.
- **Tax-sale JSON API:** TaxLienSimple documents `GET https://taxliensimple.com/api/v1/parcels`, `Authorization: Bearer …`, and `state`, `county`, and `family` filters. Set the token in `TAXLIENSIMPLE_API_KEY`, use that environment-variable name for the feed, and map only fields actually returned. Its published parcel schema does not guarantee a city field; records without the required address/city/state are explicitly skipped rather than assigned an invented location.
- **Commercial parcel/insurance providers:** Regrid and insurer quote platforms require a separate account and appropriate use/redistribution rights. A JSON API feed can be configured only after the provider grants access and its endpoint/response schema are known.

### Public endpoint checks

The following direct public endpoints were queried on **2026-10-19** for metadata, a count-only result, and one record through the matching application connector. They are shown in the county coverage view as tested endpoints, not as enabled or production-ready feeds:

- [Jefferson County, Alabama parcels](https://jccgis.jccal.org/server/rest/services/Basemap/Parcels/MapServer/0) ([ArcGIS item](https://www.arcgis.com/home/item.html?id=ac6ec7f4f83d4715a45acc8990b8b8c8)) — 410,119 records; a one-record connector query succeeded. The county says the layer is generated nightly from Addressing, Tax Assessor, and Board of Equalization data and has address/city/state plus an `AssdValue` field. Its text `MaintDate` field's maximum observed value was September 9, 2026 and is not documented as an update cursor; the publisher also disclaims accuracy warranties. This is a parcel inventory, not a distressed-lead feed, and the current 5,000-record limit/no incremental cursor prevents a complete daily county sync.
- [Mobile County, Alabama tax parcels](https://services8.arcgis.com/HND1NcQt6vgOGn1z/arcgis/rest/services/MCRC_Public_Parcels/FeatureServer/0) — 214,040 records. The publisher describes the layer as tax-purpose parcel data. The tested layer has situs address, city, and state fields, but no assessed-value field; commercial-use rights and a daily update cadence are not documented in the item metadata.
- [Benton County, Arkansas parcels](https://services1.arcgis.com/G4bYaaas92zuKaUR/arcgis/rest/services/Benton_County_Parcels/FeatureServer/0) — 174,765 records. The publisher says it is used exclusively for member-city GIS operations and is updated monthly. It must not be used commercially without written authorization; it has assessment fields but no situs city/state fields.
- [Franklin County, Ohio parcel boundaries](https://gis.franklincountyohio.gov/hosting/rest/services/ParcelFeatures/Parcel_Features/MapServer/0) ([ArcGIS item](https://www.arcgis.com/home/item.html?id=3b1c75018fca40c8b24083fd48197e74)) — 494,902 records; a one-record connector query succeeded. The official auditor item recommends its separate direct-download site for the full dataset and provides `SITEADDRESS`, `ZIPCD`, and `TOTVALUEBASE`, but no site-city field or refresh cadence. The layer response cap is 3,000 and the publisher advises consulting the primary source and disclaims legal responsibility; the current connector cannot safely backfill or incrementally sync it as a daily acquisition feed.
- [Ohio statewide parcels](https://services2.arcgis.com/MlJ0G8iWUyC7jAmu/arcgis/rest/services/OhioStatewidePacels_full_view/FeatureServer/0) — 6,313,610 records, with a county field. This is shown for the other 87 Ohio counties; Franklin County has the county-specific endpoint above. The public-view item describes county-maintained parcel and assessment records but says the dataset was updated January 21, 2024. The tested layer lacks assessed-value and situs-city fields; CAMA data is related separately. It is not a verified daily incremental source.
- [Harris County, Texas HCAD parcels](https://www.gis.hctx.net/arcgishcpid/rest/services/HCAD/Parcels/MapServer/0) ([ArcGIS item](https://www.arcgis.com/home/item.html?id=c00366c724dc47e3a550fa494d214463)) — 1,551,736 records; a one-record query through the application connector succeeded. The publisher says it is updated bi-weekly and intended for locating property, not as an official tax designation; appraised-value and tax-value fields are present but should not be treated as authoritative tax records. The layer's response cap is 2,000 records, the address is split across fields, there is no site-state field, and the connector lacks incremental sync, so it is not safe to enable as a daily full-county feed.
- [City of Dallas code violations](https://www.dallasopendata.com/resource/xrzj-c8ez.json) — 135,257 records. The City of Dallas describes the observations as residential violations from January 2015 through June 2017; the dataset was last updated in 2018. It is historical, city-scoped rather than county-wide, and lacks mapped city/state fields, so it is not a current daily lead feed.
- [City of Cincinnati code enforcement](https://data.cincinnati-oh.gov/resource/cncm-znd6.json) — 771,669 records; the dataset was updated October 19, 2026 and includes complaints, inspections, and violations. Its `full_address`/city-county fields do not provide separate city and state values, and the current connector needs a source-update cursor to avoid repeatedly taking only the first 5,000 records.
- [Cincinnati Private Lot Abatement Program](https://data.cincinnati-oh.gov/resource/pk9w-99n6.json) — 23,901 records; updated October 19, 2026. It provides a more focused code-enforcement subset but is still city-scoped, lacks separate city/state fields, and is not yet safe for daily incremental ingestion.
- [St. Louis County, Missouri parcels](https://services2.arcgis.com/w657bnjzrjguNyOy/arcgis/rest/services/STLCO_STC_Parcels_Revised/FeatureServer/0) ([ArcGIS item](https://www.arcgis.com/home/item.html?id=06d43012de4a484ab004f5b34ea0e8e8)) — 400,796 records; a one-record connector query succeeded. The county GIS item attributes the data to the Assessor and includes parcel address, municipality, and `TOTASSMT` fields, but its maximum observed `TAXYR` is 2023 and no refresh cadence is documented. The publisher warns users to validate with the primary source; the layer caps queries at 2,000 records and the connector has no incremental cursor, so it is not a current daily feed.
- [Kansas City, Missouri property violations](https://data.kcmo.org/resource/vq3e-m9ge.json) — 174,999 records, but the dataset was last updated April 30, 2026. It is limited to Kansas City rather than all of Jackson County, and the publisher does not document a daily update cadence. Aggregate validation found one future-dated record and 35 missing violation dates.
- [City of Midland, Texas code-enforcement inspections](https://services.arcgis.com/0H6bQdxd9223gQB5/arcgis/rest/services/Inspections/FeatureServer/0) — 293,437 records, but the latest row edit was March 4, 2026. It is city-scoped, does not expose separate city/state fields, and is not a current daily feed.

### County-specific source review inventory

The list below tracks counties with a county-specific source block in [`backend/app/jurisdictions.py`](./backend/app/jurisdictions.py). “Source-reviewed” means that county-specific links and availability/usage limitations were recorded for the five listed categories; it does **not** certify a complete current feed, permission for automated collection, or a verified refresh schedule. A provider that is unavailable or restricts automation is still listed as reviewed, with that limitation recorded.

- **Alabama — 67/67 source-reviewed:** Autauga, Baldwin, Barbour, Bibb, Blount, Bullock, Butler, Calhoun, Chambers, Cherokee, Chilton, Choctaw, Clarke, Clay, Cleburne, Coffee, Colbert, Conecuh, Coosa, Covington, Crenshaw, Cullman, Dale, Dallas, DeKalb, Elmore, Escambia, Etowah, Fayette, Franklin, Geneva, Greene, Hale, Henry, Houston, Jackson, Jefferson, Lamar, Lauderdale, Lawrence, Lee, Limestone, Lowndes, Macon, Madison, Marengo, Marion, Marshall, Mobile, Monroe, Montgomery, Morgan, Perry, Pickens, Pike, Randolph, Russell, Shelby, St. Clair, Sumter, Talladega, Tallapoosa, Tuscaloosa, Walker, Washington, Wilcox, Winston.
- **Arkansas — 75/75 source-reviewed:** Arkansas, Ashley, Baxter, Benton, Boone, Bradley, Calhoun, Carroll, Chicot, Clark, Clay, Cleburne, Cleveland, Columbia, Conway, Craighead, Crawford, Crittenden, Cross, Dallas, Desha, Drew, Faulkner, Franklin, Fulton, Garland, Grant, Greene, Hempstead, Hot Spring, Howard, Independence, Izard, Jackson, Jefferson, Johnson, Lafayette, Lawrence, Lee, Lincoln, Little River, Logan, Lonoke, Madison, Marion, Miller, Mississippi, Monroe, Montgomery, Nevada, Newton, Ouachita, Perry, Phillips, Pike, Poinsett, Polk, Pope, Prairie, Pulaski, Randolph, Saline, Scott, Searcy, Sebastian, Sevier, Sharp, St. Francis, Stone, Union, Van Buren, Washington, White, Woodruff, Yell.
- **Arkansas link validation (2026-10-30):** All 75 county-specific source blocks were rechecked. The Association of Arkansas Counties and Commissioner of State Lands links load. The 63 ARCountyData assessor links (county-specific Assessor search pages) returned HTTP 429 or 403 (a Cloudflare bot challenge on sampled pages), so they could not be validated by automated request; they remain unverified and should be checked in a browser. - **Missouri — 10/115 source-reviewed:** Adair, Andrew, Atchison, Audrain, Barry, Barton, Bates, Benton, Bollinger, Boone.
- The Missouri county-specific source-discovery links were checked on **2026-10-23** for all 115 county-equivalents: 114 returned HTTP 200 with the county name; Platte County's site returned HTTP 403. This confirms link reachability only, not an actual tax-sale list or feed. County profiles are office/contact directories, while Missouri Public Notices terms prohibit scraping, automation, and commercial reuse.
- **Ohio — 0/88 full five-category source reviews.** The official [County Auditors' Association of Ohio directory](https://caao.org/auditors-directory/) has county-specific profiles; all 88 profile URLs returned HTTP 200 and each page title matched the county on **2026-10-23**. These are auditor contact/website listings, not parcel feeds. The tax-sale, code-enforcement, foreclosure, and pre-foreclosure sources still need county-specific review.
- **Texas — 191/254 full five-category source reviews:** Anderson, Andrews, Angelina, Aransas, Archer, Armstrong, Atascosa, Austin, Bailey, Bandera, Bastrop, Baylor, Bee, Bell, Bexar, Borden, Bosque, Bowie, Brazoria, Brazos, Brewster, Briscoe, Brooks, Brown, Burleson, Caldwell, Calhoun, Callahan, Cameron, Camp, Carson, Cass, Castro, Chambers, Cherokee, Childress, Clay, Cochran, Coke, Coleman, Collin, Collingsworth, Colorado, Comal, Comanche, Concho, Cooke, Coryell, Cottle, Crane, Crockett, Crosby, Culberson, Dallam, Dallas, Dawson, Deaf Smith, Delta, Denton, DeWitt, Donley, Duval, Eastland, Ector, Edwards, El Paso, Ellis, Erath, Falls, Fannin, Fayette, Fisher, Floyd, Foard, Fort Bend, Franklin, Freestone, Frio, Gaines, Galveston, Garza, Gillespie, Glasscock, Goliad, Gonzales, Gray, Grayson, Gregg, Grimes, Guadalupe, Hale, Hall, Hamilton, Hansford, Hardeman, Hardin, Harris, Harrison, Hartley, Haskell, Hays, Hemphill, Henderson, Hidalgo, Hill, Houston, Howard, Hudspeth, Hunt, Hutchinson, Irion, Jack, Jackson, Jasper, Jeff Davis, Jim Hogg, Jim Wells, Johnson, Jones, Karnes, Kaufman, Kendall, Kenedy, Kent, Kerr, Kimble, King, Kinney, Kleberg, Knox, La Salle, Lamar, Lamb, Lampasas, Lavaca, Lee, Leon, Liberty, Limestone, Lipscomb, Live Oak, Llano, Loving, Lubbock, Lynn, McCulloch, McLennan, McMullen, Madison, Marion, Martin, Mason, Matagorda, Maverick, Medina, Menard, Midland, Milam, Mills, Mitchell, Montague, Montgomery, Moore, Morris, Motley, Nacogdoches, Navarro, Newton, Nolan, Nueces, Ochiltree, Oldham, Orange, Palo Pinto, Panola, Parker, Parmer, Pecos, Polk, Potter, Presidio, Rains, Randall, Reagan, Real, Red River, Reeves, Refugio, Roberts, Robertson, and Rockwall. The five-category county-specific inventory currently covers **343 of 599** priority county-equivalents. The Ohio and remaining Texas assessor-directory checks above cover 151 additional county profiles in one category only; they do not count as full county source reviews or establish parcel-feed readiness. Separate, narrower endpoint checks are documented above for Jefferson and Mobile Counties, Alabama; Benton County, Arkansas; St. Louis County, Missouri; Kansas City, Missouri (city scope within Jackson County); and Midland, Texas (city scope). Those endpoint checks do not imply full source review of each county or municipality.

### Foreclosure and tax-sale source checks

- **Anderson County, Texas (2026-11-02):** The [County Tax Assessor-Collector page](https://www.andersoncountytx.gov/page/County.assessor.collector) links a property-tax portal that failed direct retrieval. The [Appraisal District site](https://www.andersoncad.net/) returned HTTP 200 but displayed browser-download guidance rather than a usable search page. The County's “Foreclosure Notices” link opens a [general public-record search](https://anderson.tx.publicsearch.us/), not a dedicated sale list; no export or refresh cadence was confirmed. No countywide code-enforcement or pre-foreclosure feed was found.
- **Andrews County, Texas (2026-11-02):** The [Appraisal District](https://andrewscad.org/) links an interactive eSearch property portal. The County Clerk's [Notice of Trustee Sales page](https://www.co.andrews.tx.us/186/Notice-of-Trustee-Sales) says it covers the last 90 days and lists dated PDF notices, but recent November/December DocumentCenter links returned 404 when downloaded, so their contents and sale type could not be verified. The Tax Assessor-Collector route returned 404 and the property-tax payment portal timed out; no separate tax-sale list was found. The Clerk's official-record search is not a verified pre-foreclosure index, and no countywide code-enforcement feed was identified.
- **Angelina County, Texas (2026-11-02):** The [official Comptroller directory](https://comptroller.texas.gov/taxes/property-tax/county-directory/angelina.php) lists the County Appraisal District, but its website and the County Tax Office page timed out in this check. A linked [delinquent-tax-roll ZIP](https://www.angelinacounty.net/files/pdf/tax/Delinquent/trwfile.110125.zip) downloaded successfully; its read-me says amounts were current as of November 4, 2025, so it is stale and is not an upcoming sale list. The County's [November 3, 2026 foreclosure notices](https://www.angelinacounty.net/events/notice-of-foreclosure-sale-22/) include individual PDFs; a four-page sample identifies a deed-of-trust lender foreclosure, not a tax sale. The County Clerk page timed out, and no countywide code-enforcement or pre-foreclosure feed was confirmed.
- **Aransas County, Texas (2026-11-02):** The [Appraisal District's eSearch](https://esearch.aransascad.org/) is an interactive property portal, not a verified bulk export. The [Tax Assessor-Collector page](https://www.aransascountytx.gov/taxac/) links December 2026 Tax Sale Information, Resale List, and Notice of Sale PDFs; the sale is scheduled for December 1, 2026, and the County says sales normally occur every three or four months. The linked auction portal returned HTTP 403. The Clerk's [foreclosure-notice page](https://www.aransascountytx.gov/clerk/forcnotices.php) lists three individual PDFs whose direct links returned 404; its interactive Tyler search portal returned HTTP 200. No countywide code-enforcement or pre-foreclosure feed was found.
- **Archer County, Texas (2026-11-02):** The [Appraisal District property search](https://www.archercad.org/home) returned HTTP 200; its separate Records | Data | Reports page exposed no downloadable dataset. The County Tax Assessor-Collector page links an interactive property-tax search, not a tax-sale list. The [foreclosure/trustee notice page](https://www.co.archer.tx.us/page/archer.Foreclosure.Trustee.Notice) lists scheduled PDFs; November 3 and December 1, 2026 samples downloaded as three-page files but had no extractable text, so tax-versus-lender sale type could not be confirmed. No authorized daily assessor, tax-sale, code-enforcement, foreclosure, or pre-foreclosure feed was established.
- **Alabama foreclosure notices:** [Alabama Public Notice](https://www.alabamapublicnotices.com/) has a Foreclosures category and county search; its homepage says it receives daily uploads from participating newspapers. That does not establish complete county coverage. Its [Terms of Use](https://www.alabamapublicnotices.com/Terms-of-Use.aspx) prohibit scraping, spidering, automated collection, and commercial exploitation. Do not ingest without written authorization. Foreclosure-sale notices are not pre-foreclosure/default notices; no complete statewide pre-foreclosure feed was verified.
- **Arkansas tax sales:** The [Commissioner of State Lands public auction catalog](https://cosl.org/Home/Contents) and [post-auction listings](https://auction.cosl.org/Auctions/ListingsView) are statewide sources for delinquent land certified to the state. The [County Reports selector](https://cosl.org/Countyfiles/CountyReports) lists all 75 Arkansas counties and years 2013–2026. For Arkansas County, the 2025 Tax Turnback page listed monthly Sales and Redemption reports, with PDF and XLS links; a sample October Sales XLS download returned HTTP 200 (17,408 bytes) on **2026-10-26**. This confirms manual monthly report downloads, not the contents or parcel-level completeness of those reports. On **2026-10-19**, the post-auction grid reported 3,036 parcels in 53 counties, with ongoing bids in 40 counties. The scheduled November 10, 2026 Sharp County catalog link said to check back closer to the sale date. The grid route is undocumented, listings do not include situs addresses, and permission for automated weekly downloads was not verified; the catalog is not yet confirmed as a countywide, downloadable weekly feed.
- **Harris County, Texas tax sales (2026-10-26):** The official [Harris County Tax Office sale page](https://www.hctax.net/Property/TaxSales/Index) links its [Tax Sale Property Listings](https://www.hctax.net/Property/listings/taxsalelisting) for monthly delinquent-property auctions. The live listing is HTML with account/cause numbers, adjudged values, and minimum bids; no CSV, XLS, PDF, or export control was found. The County says the list is updated regularly but may not reflect final sale-day availability and recommends additional sources for a complete list. This is a usable public list for manual review, not a verified weekly download/feed; automated-use permission was not found.
- **Texas Public Notices:** The [statewide newspaper-notice search](https://www.texaspublicnotices.com/) is not a tax-auction calendar. Its county filter showed 249 named counties plus “Unknown” on **2026-10-19**, omitting Glasscock, King, Loving, McMullen, and Terrell. Terms checked on **2026-10-26** allow personal, non-commercial viewing and downloading or printing individual pages, but prohibit incorporating content into a database, compilation, archive, or cache and prohibit screen/database scraping or other automated collection. Personal use does not authorize automated weekly list ingestion; use manual page-level access or obtain written authorization before any integration.
- **Arkansas County source check (2026-10-19; COSL report follow-up 2026-10-26):** The [Association of Arkansas Counties profile](https://www.arcounties.org/counties/arkansas/) lists County Judge, Clerk, Circuit Clerk, and Sheriff contacts. The county-specific [ARCountyData page](https://www.arcountydata.com/county.asp?county=Arkansas&s=R) says the Assessor sponsors a free public property-record search; it is an interactive third-party service, not a documented daily feed. Its [Terms of Use](https://www.arcountydata.com/terms.asp) describe parcel data as assessor working papers and restrict copying or reproducing proprietary material without express written consent; no authorized export/API or refresh cadence was verified. The State Land Office [Real Estate page](https://cosl.org/Home/RealEstate) confirms public auctions of delinquent property certified to the State and links the [auction catalog](https://cosl.org/Home/Contents). The catalog exposed no Arkansas County-specific upcoming sale date or parcel catalog; the separate County Reports archive offers monthly Tax Turnback Sales and Redemption downloads, whose parcel-level contents and completeness remain unverified. The County profile lists Sheriff contacts but no sale calendar. [Arkansas Public Notices](https://www.arkansaspublicnotices.com/) offers county-searchable newspaper foreclosure notices, not official publication, and says newspapers are not required to upload every notice. Its [Terms of Use](https://www.arkansaspublicnotices.com/Terms-of-Use.aspx) permit personal, non-commercial access but separately prohibit copying or replicating site functionality; personal use alone does not establish permission for automated collection or building a searchable copy. The official Arkansas Judiciary [Case Search](https://caseinfo.arcourts.gov/opad) page returned general navigation tips; no Arkansas County-specific default/lis-pendens feed, bulk/API, update cadence, or authorized automated access was verified. No countywide property-level code-enforcement feed was identified; municipal authorities remain unverified. No authorized scheduled assessor, tax-sale, code-enforcement, foreclosure, or pre-foreclosure feed was established.

- **Arkansas foreclosure notices:** [Arkansas Public Notices](https://www.arkansaspublicnotices.com/) has a Foreclosure search category and says newspapers upload notices daily, searchable by county. It also says the database is not a substitute for official newspaper publication and that newspapers are not required to upload notices. Its [Terms of Use](https://www.arkansaspublicnotices.com/Terms-of-Use.aspx) limit use to personal, non-commercial purposes and prohibit copying or replication. It is not usable for this commercial platform without written permission and does not verify complete county coverage.
- **Arkansas pre-foreclosure:** No complete statewide source was verified. A foreclosure-sale notice search is not a verified pre-foreclosure/default-notice feed.
- **Missouri tax-sale and foreclosure notices:** [Missouri Public Notices](https://www.mopublicnotices.com/) is maintained by the Missouri Press Association, describes a statewide newspaper-notice compilation, and exposes separate Tax Sale, Foreclosure, and Sheriff's Sales searches. Its [Terms of Use](https://www.mopublicnotices.com/Terms-of-Use.aspx) limit access to personal, non-commercial page viewing and prohibit scraping, automated collection, and commercial reuse; they also disclaim responsibility for timely notice publication. This is a manual discovery source, not a verified complete collector auction calendar or permitted daily feed. Missouri county-office directory links were checked for all 115 county-equivalents on **2026-10-19**: 113 Missouri Association of Counties profiles, the official Platte County site, and the City of St. Louis site all returned HTTP 200 and matched the county/city name. These pages identify local officials but do not provide a verified tax-sale feed. No complete statewide pre-foreclosure/default-notice feed was verified.
- **Ohio foreclosure notices:** [Public Notices Ohio](https://www.publicnoticesohio.com/) is operated by the Ohio News Media Association and has Foreclosure and Sheriff's Sales searches; its county filter listed all 88 Ohio counties on **2026-10-23**. Its homepage says newspapers upload notices daily, but also says its goal is to carry every Ohio public notice in the future, so complete coverage is not established. Its [Terms of Use](https://www.publicnoticesohio.com/Terms-of-Use.aspx) limit use to personal, non-commercial use. Do not use it as a commercial feed without written authorization. The State-contracted [Realauction Franklin County portal](https://franklin.sheriffsaleauction.ohio.gov/) exposed a selector for 87 other counties; a Medina-to-Franklin jump worked, but the selector does not prove every county destination is live or has current listings. The [Franklin portal's User Agreement](https://franklin.sheriffsaleauction.ohio.gov/index.cfm?zaction=home&zmethod=agreement) says the state contracted with Realauction to provide foreclosure bidding services in each county. For Franklin, the license limits use of information to purchasing property and prohibits use, alteration, sale, or distribution without express written consent; county-specific agreements must be reviewed and written authorization obtained before any ingestion. The [County Treasurers Association of Ohio directory](https://www.ohiocountytreasurers.org/aws/CTAO/pt/sp/layout_directory-treasurer?get_content_from_session=1) links a [June 1, 2026 PDF directory](https://www.ohiocountytreasurers.org/aws/CTAO/asset_manager/get_file/952428?ver=1) with treasurer profiles for all 88 Ohio counties; only 48 entries include a website field. A link check on 2026-10-19 reached 41 of these websites: 39 returned HTTP 200 to HEAD, while GET confirmed Ashtabula and Franklin despite HEAD 405/403. Five remained blocked with HTTP 403 (Greene, Lawrence, Licking, Montgomery, Portage); Fairfield failed DNS resolution and Monroe returned 404. These are office contacts, not a tax-sale schedule. The [Buckeye State Sheriffs' Association contact map](https://buckeyesheriffs.org/contact-your-sheriff/) is also only a directory: the association says local sheriffs exclusively operate their offices and it holds no foreclosure or sheriff-sale information. County tax-sale, foreclosure, and pre-foreclosure feeds remain unverified.
- **Texas foreclosure and tax-sale notices:** [Texas Public Notices](https://www.texaspublicnotices.com/) is a Texas Press Association statewide newspaper-notice database with county filters. A manual foreclosure keyword search returned a Smith County notice of sale, but the selector displayed only 249 named county options plus “Unknown” on **2026-10-19**; Glasscock, King, Loving, McMullen, and Terrell were absent. The portal is not a verified tax-auction calendar or proof of complete county coverage. Its [Terms of Use](https://www.texaspublicnotices.com/Terms-of-Use.aspx) restrict content to personal, non-commercial use and prohibit screen/database scraping and other automated collection; the terms state liquidated damages of $100 per notice or $10,000 per incident. Do not scrape or commercially ingest it. No complete statewide pre-foreclosure/default-notice feed was verified.
- **Texas county discovery directory:** [Texas Counties](https://texascounties.org/counties) identifies itself as independent and unofficial, lists all 254 counties, and links to local property-tax resources. Its [data-status page](https://texascounties.org/data-status/) showed burn-ban data stale and said other refresh jobs were not published when checked; it is a discovery aid, not an official sale source or verified feed.

These endpoint checks prove only that the listed service responded and returned data at the time tested. They do not certify commercial-use rights, completeness, update guarantees, or that the connector can safely use the layer for daily acquisition leads. No feed was automatically enabled. ArcGIS and Socrata catalog surveys did not establish production-ready county-wide direct feeds for tax sales, code enforcement, foreclosure, or pre-foreclosure. Some city-level code APIs responded but are stale or still require location normalization and incremental-cursor support, so they remain unconfigured. A reviewed code-enforcement candidate returned zero records, and a historic foreclosure layer lacked property addresses, so neither was treated as a live lead feed.

API-feed retrieval is capped at 5,000 records per run and 10 MB per response page. Stable source IDs are used to upsert property-feed records; records without IDs use normalized address/city/state as a fallback. Invalid or incomplete property rows are counted as skipped and shown in sync results. Raw source-record feeds retain the downloaded CSV rows or text-extracted PDF pages separately. A configured feed is not evidence that the provider covers a whole state or refreshes daily; verify coverage, terms, and publication cadence with the source. For ArcGIS and Socrata feeds, the executor requests only fields named in the field mapping and optional record-ID field; it does not request every attribute from the service.

The priority-county registry is generated from the U.S. Census Bureau national county-equivalent FIPS reference and covers 599 counties/county-equivalents across Alabama, Arkansas, Missouri, Ohio, and Texas. The source directory links to the Alabama Department of Revenue county assessment pages, Association of Arkansas Counties county profiles, County Auditors' Association of Ohio directory, Missouri State Tax Commission/Missouri Association of Counties directories, and Texas Comptroller/Texas Association of Counties/Texas Municipal League resources. Foreclosure and pre-foreclosure discovery links lead to state judiciary, county-office, or association resources plus county-specific searches; they are not claims that each county exposes an API, complete sale calendar, or pre-foreclosure notice feed. Foreclosure and notice publication processes vary by state and county, and some listings require an authorized commercial provider. The daily executor only retrieves feeds explicitly configured and verified by the operator; discovery links are not scraped as feeds. Code enforcement is often municipal, and tax-sale feeds are administered locally or require a provider account. The county coverage view intentionally labels those local endpoints as unconfigured until an actual feed has been verified and registered.

FBI Crime Data Explorer data is reported by law-enforcement agency and requires an API key plus an explicit, verified agency/geography mapping; it is not a block-level crime feed. Import official FBI summaries or configure an appropriate local government feed and map its documented rate to `violent_crime_rate_per_100k`. HUD/PHA coverage, payment standards, waitlist status, and open-voucher counts are distinct facts; the system does not infer voucher counts from PHA participation or waitlist status.

There is no comprehensive public nationwide property-level insurance quote API. Insurance premiums remain quote- or CSV-based unless a licensed carrier/broker provider is separately selected and authorized. The system does not fill missing insurance costs with an unsupported estimate. Nationwide parcel assessment, code-enforcement, and tax-sale coverage is also not a universal feed: configure the relevant jurisdiction or a licensed data provider for each market.

## Run locally on Windows

Requires Python 3.11+ and Node.js 18+.

From the repository root, install the Python package and development test dependency:

```powershell
python -m pip install -e ".[dev]"
```

Start the API (SQLite is used by default):

```powershell
python -m alembic upgrade head
python -m uvicorn app.main:app --app-dir backend --reload --port 8001
```

In another PowerShell window, install and start the React client:

```powershell
Set-Location frontend
npm install
npm run dev
```

Open the Vite URL (normally `http://localhost:5173`). FastAPI API documentation is at `http://127.0.0.1:8001/docs`.

For Census enrichment, provide a property county FIPS (two state digits followed by three county digits) and use **Refresh county population from Census ACS**. Set `CENSUS_ACS_YEAR` to another available ACS 5-year vintage if needed; the default is 2024. Census requests require outbound internet access.

## PostgreSQL / PostGIS

Install PostGIS on the PostgreSQL server, configure `DATABASE_URL`, and run migrations before starting the API:

```powershell
$env:DATABASE_URL = "postgresql+psycopg://USER:PASSWORD@HOST:5432/DATABASE"
python -m alembic upgrade head
python -m uvicorn app.main:app --app-dir backend --reload --port 8001
```

The API verifies/installs the PostGIS extension and maintains the point geometry from latitude/longitude on startup. The database role therefore needs permission to create the extension if it is not already installed. Set `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` to a comma-separated list before deployment.

## Checks

```powershell
python -m pytest
Set-Location frontend
npm run build
```

This is an internal MVP foundation, not a production-hosted financial product. It has no authentication or user/tenant isolation; do not expose it to the public internet without adding access control, HTTPS, and deployment-specific secret management. Estimates omit financing, utilities, HOA fees, closing costs, and property-specific rent-reasonableness review.
